I am the river warrior—Tom Aiken keeps saying I am—and I’m not scared. We’ll walk up the river in the dark and hook eels by their mouths and then swing them onto the rocks. Tom Aiken says he’ll go first and then I can try. “Watch the water carefully,” Tom says. “Point if you see one.” A bit of moon is high in the sky now, and the air is getting cold. Tom Aiken turns to us again and gives a big grin before he takes a different grip on the gaff. River warriors don’t care about water in their gumboots. Without thinking, I kick the gumboots off and pull off my socks with one hand. The socks have fire trucks on them. River warriors don’t wear socks with fire trucks on them. My feet are bare now. I see an eel swimming ahead. I creep up behind the swimming-slow eel. It comes up to the waistband of my shorts. Beth and Aunty Kat have stopped following us. I search the dark water for the eel but I can’t see him.
